# Research: subset-b-000585

Grouped research for ceph-client Linux media Devicetree binding YAML schemas covering camera sensors, serializers/deserializers, video bridges, analog decoders, radio/IR receivers, JPEG/video codecs, and MediaTek or Marvell media processing blocks. Each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max9286.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max9286.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim Integrated Quad GMSL Deserializer`, a automotive serializer/deserializer and camera-link bridge binding. The MAX9286 deserializer receives video data on up to 4 Gigabit Multimedia Serial Links (GMSL) and outputs them on a CSI-2 D-PHY port using up to 4 data lanes. In addition to video data, the GMSL links carry a bidirectional control channel that encapsulates I2C messages. The MAX9286 forwards all I2C traffic not addressed to itself to the other side of the links, where a GMSL serializer will output it on a local I2C bus. In the other direction all I2C traffic received over GMSL by the MAX9286 is output on the local I2C bus. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `maxim,max9286`. Top-level properties are `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `compatible`, `reg`, `poc-supply`, `enable-gpios`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `maxim,bus-width`, `maxim,i2c-remote-bus-hz`, `maxim,reverse-channel-microvolt`, `maxim,gpio-poc`, `ports`, `i2c-mux`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `ports`, `i2c-mux`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `data-lanes`, `i2c-mux`, `maxim,gpio-poc`, `poc-supply`, `port`, `port@4`, `ports`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `#address-cells` enables validated child nodes or provider specifiers; `#size-cells` enables validated child nodes or provider specifiers; `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `poc-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `enable-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `#gpio-cells` enables validated child nodes or provider specifiers; `ports` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology; `i2c-mux` models remote I2C access behind serializers or deserializers. Patterned child schemas are `^port[0-3]-poc-supply$`; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`, `port@0`, `port@1`, `port@2`, `port@3`, `port@4`, `ports`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Jacopo Mondi <jacopo+renesas@jmondi.org>, Kieran Bingham <kieran.bingham+renesas@ideasonboard.com>, Laurent Pinchart <laurent.pinchart+renesas@ideasonboard.com>, Niklas Söderlund <niklas.soderlund+renesas@ragnatech.se>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/max9286.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/condor-common.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r8a77970-eagle.dts`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 14 top-level properties and 22 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max9286.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max9286.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max96712.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max96712.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Quad GMSL2 to CSI-2 Deserializer with GMSL1 Compatibility`, a automotive serializer/deserializer and camera-link bridge binding. The MAX96712 deserializer converts GMSL2 or GMSL1 serial inputs into MIPI CSI-2 D-PHY or C-PHY formatted outputs. The device allows each link to simultaneously transmit bidirectional control-channel data while forward video transmissions are in progress. The MAX96712 can accommodate as many as four remotely located sensors using industry-standard coax or STP interconnects. Each GMSL2 serial link operates at a fixed rate of 3Gbps or 6Gbps in the forward direction and 187.5Mbps in the reverse direction. In GMSL1 mode, the MAX96712 can be paired with first-generation 3.12Gbps or 1.5Gbps GMSL1 serializers or operate up to 3.12Gbps with GMSL2 serializers in GMSL1 mode. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. **Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/maxim,max96714.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Maxim MAX96714 GMSL2 to CSI-2 Deserializer`, a automotive serializer/deserializer and camera-link bridge binding. The MAX96714 deserializer converts GMSL2 serial inputs into MIPI CSI-2 D-PHY formatted output. The device allows the GMSL2 link to simultaneously transmit bidirectional control-channel data while forward video transmissions are in progress. The MAX96714 can connect to one remotely located serializer using industry-standard coax or STP interconnects. The device cans operate in pixel or tunnel mode. In pixel mode the MAX96714 can select individual video stream, while the tunnel mode forward all the MIPI data received by the serializer. The GMSL2 serial link operates at a fixed rate of 3Gbps or 6Gbps in the forward direction and 187.5Mbps in the reverse direction. MAX96714F only supports a fixed rate of 3Gbps in the forward direction. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/mipi-ccs.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/mipi-ccs.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MIPI CCS, SMIA++ and SMIA compliant camera sensors`, a camera image sensor binding. CCS (Camera Command Set) is a raw Bayer camera sensor standard defined by the MIPI Alliance; see <URL:https://www.mipi.org/specifications/camera-command-set>. SMIA (Standard Mobile Imaging Architecture) is an image sensor standard defined jointly by Nokia and ST. SMIA++, defined by Nokia, is an extension of that. More detailed documentation can be found in Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/video-interfaces.txt . It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vana-supply`, `vcore-supply`, `vio-supply`, `clocks`, `clock-frequency`, `reset-gpios`, `flash-leds`, `lens-focus`, `rotation`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`; required properties found across nested schemas include `bus-type`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `data-lanes`, `link-frequencies`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `vana-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vcore-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vio-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. **Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. The TDA19971/73 are HDMI video receivers. The TDA19971 Video port output pins can be used as follows: - RGB 8bit per color (24 bits total): R[11:4] B[11:4] G[11:4] - YUV444 8bit per color (24 bits total): Y[11:4] Cr[11:4] Cb[11:4] - YUV422 semi-planar 8bit per component (16 bits total): Y[11:4] CbCr[11:4] - YUV422 semi-planar 10bit per component (20 bits total): Y[11:2] CbCr[11:2] - YUV422 semi-planar 12bit per component (24 bits total): - Y[11:0] CbCr[11:0] - YUV422 BT656 8bit per component (8 bits total): YCbCr[11:4] (2-cycles) - YUV422 BT656 10bit per component (10 bits total): YCbCr[11:2] (2-cycles) - YUV422 BT656 12bit per component (12 bits total): YCbCr[11:0] (2-cycles) The TDA19973 Video port output pins can be used as follows: - RGB 12bit... # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,og0ve1b.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,og0ve1b.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `OmniVision OG0VE1B Image Sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. OmniVision OG0VE1B image sensor is a low power consuming monochrome image sensor. The sensor is controlled over a serial camera control bus protocol (SCCB), the widest supported image size is 640x480 at 120 frames per second rate, data output format is 8/10-bit RAW transferred over one-lane MIPI D-PHY at up to 800 Mbps. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `ovti,og0ve1b`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `reset-gpios`, `strobe-gpios`, `avdd-supply`, `dovdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `link-frequencies`, `port`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `strobe-gpios` describes control/status GPIO wiring; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Vladimir Zapolskiy <vladimir.zapolskiy@linaro.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/og0ve1b.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,og0ve1b.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,og0ve1b.yaml -->

Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov08x40.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov08x40.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Omnivision OV08X40 CMOS Sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. The Omnivision OV08X40 is a 9.2 megapixel, CMOS image sensor which supports: - Automatic black level calibration (ABLC) - Programmable controls for frame rate, mirror and flip, binning, cropping and windowing - Output formats 10-bit 4C RGB RAW, 10-bit Bayer RAW - 4-lane MIPI D-PHY TX @ 1 Gbps per lane - 2-lane MPIP D-PHY TX @ 2 Gbps per lane - Dynamic defect pixel cancellation - Standard SCCB command interface It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `ovti,ov08x40`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `avdd-supply`, `dovdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `reset-gpios`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `data-lanes`, `link-frequencies`, `port`, `reg`, `remote-endpoint`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov2680.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Omnivision OV2680 CMOS Sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. The OV2680 color sensor is a low voltage, high performance 1/5 inch UXGA (2 megapixel) CMOS image sensor that provides a single-chip UXGA (1600 x 1200) camera. It provides full-frame, sub-sampled, or windowed 10-bit images in various formats via the control of the Serial Camera Control Bus (SCCB) interface. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `avdd-supply`, `dovdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `avdd-supply`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `data-lanes`, `dovdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `link-frequencies`, `port`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `enable-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Himanshu Bhavani <himanshu.bhavani@siliconsignals.io>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/ov2735.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov2735.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov2735.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov4689.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov4689.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Omnivision OV4689 CMOS`, a camera image sensor binding. The Omnivision OV4689 is a high performance, 1/3-inch, 4 megapixel image sensor. Ihis chip supports high frame rate speeds up to 90 fps at 2688x1520 resolution. It is programmable through an I2C interface, and sensor output is sent via 1/2/4 lane MIPI CSI-2 connection. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6qdl-pico.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/nxp/imx/imx6qdl-wandboard.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/aistarvision-mipi-adapter-2.1.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/rz-smarc-cru-csi-ov5645.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 10 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov5645.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov5647.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov5647.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Omnivision OV5647 raw image sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. The OV5647 is a raw image sensor with MIPI CSI-2 and CCP2 image data interfaces and CCI (I2C compatible) control bus. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `ovti,ov5647`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `pwdn-gpios`, `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `dovdd-supply`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `port`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `pwdn-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Dave Stevenson <dave.stevenson@raspberrypi.com>, Jacopo Mondi <jacopo@jmondi.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/ov5647.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 8 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov5647.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

The Omnivision OV5675 is a high performance, 1/5-inch, 5 megapixel, CMOS image sensor that delivers 2592x1944 at 30fps. It provides full-frame, sub-sampled, and windowed 10-bit MIPI images in various formats via the Serial Camera Control Bus (SCCB) interface. This chip is programmable through I2C and two-wire SCCB. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `clock-names` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Tommaso Merciai <tomm.merciai@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/ov5693.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/ov5695.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/px30-evb.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/rockchip/rk3399-gru-scarlet.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov5693.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov8856.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov8856.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Omnivision OV8856 CMOS Sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. The Omnivision OV8856 is a high performance, 1/4-inch, 8 megapixel, CMOS image sensor that delivers 3264x2448 at 30fps. It provides full-frame, sub-sampled, and windowed 10-bit MIPI images in various formats via the Serial Camera Control Bus (SCCB) interface. This chip is programmable through I2C and two-wire SCCB. The sensor output is available via CSI-2 serial data output (up to 4-lane). It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `ovti,ov8856`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `clock-frequency`, `dovdd-supply`, `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `reset-gpios`, `orientation`, `rotation`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `dovdd-supply`, `avdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `reset-gpios`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `avdd-supply`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `dovdd-supply`, `dvdd-supply`, `link-frequencies`, `port`, `reg`, `reset-gpios`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `clock-names` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `dovdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `avdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `dvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Sakari Ailus <sakari.ailus@linux.intel.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/ov8856.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sdm845-db845c-navigation-mezzanine.dtso`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 12 top-level properties and 14 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov8856.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/ovti,ov8856.yaml -->

OV9282 sensor is an OmniVision black & white CMOS active pixel digital image sensor with an active array size of 1296H x 816V. It is programmable through I2C interface. The I2C client address is fixed to 0x60/0x70 as per sensor data sheet. Image data is sent through MIPI CSI-2. OV9281 has a different lens chief ray angle. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/samsung,s5k3m5.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/samsung,s5k3m5.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Samsung S5K3M5 Image Sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. Samsung S5K3M5 (ISOCELL 3M5) image sensor is a 13MP image sensor. The sensor is controlled over a serial camera control bus protocol, the widest supported output image frame size is 4208x3120 at 30 frames per second, data output format is RAW10 transferred over 4-lane MIPI D-PHY interface. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `samsung,s5k3m5`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `reset-gpios`, `afvdd-supply`, `vdda-supply`, `vddd-supply`, `vddio-supply`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `compatible`, `link-frequencies`, `port`, `reg`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `afvdd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vdda-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddd-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddio-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Vladimir Zapolskiy <vladimir.zapolskiy@linaro.org>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/s5k3m5.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sm8550-hdk-rear-camera-card.dtso`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/sm8550-qrd.dts`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/samsung,s5k3m5.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Svyatoslav Ryhel <clamor95@gmail.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/imx111.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 8 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/sony,imx111.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 9 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/sony,imx274.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

The Sony IMX290 is a 1/2.8-Inch CMOS Solid-state image sensor with Square Pixel, available in either mono or colour variants. It is programmable through I2C and 4-wire interfaces. The sensor output is available via CMOS logic parallel SDR output, Low voltage LVDS DDR output and CSI-2 serial data output. The CSI-2 bus is the default. No bindings have been defined for the other busses. imx290lqr is the full model identifier for the colour variant. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Paul J. Murphy <paul.j.murphy@intel.com>, Daniele Alessandrelli <daniele.alessandrelli@intel.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/imx335.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/st/stm32mp257f-ev1.dts`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 8 top-level properties and 11 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/sony,imx335.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

The Sony IMX415 is a diagonal 6.4 mm (Type 1/2.8) CMOS active pixel type solid-state image sensor with a square pixel array and 8.46 M effective pixels. This chip operates with analog 2.9 V, digital 1.1 V, and interface 1.8 V triple power supply, and has low power consumption. The IMX415 is programmable through I2C interface. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/st,st-mipid02.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/st,st-mipid02.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics MIPID02 CSI-2 to PARALLEL bridge`, a video receiver or media bus bridge binding. MIPID02 has two CSI-2 input ports, only one of those ports can be active at a time. Active port input stream will be de-serialized and its content outputted through PARALLEL output port. CSI-2 first input port is a dual lane 800Mbps per lane whereas CSI-2 second input port is a single lane 800Mbps. Both ports support clock and data lane polarity swap. First port also supports data lane swap. PARALLEL output port has a maximum width of 12 bits. Supported formats are RAW6, RAW7, RAW8, RAW10, RAW12, RGB565, RGB888, RGB444, YUV420 8-bit, YUV422 8-bit and YUV420 10-bit. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/st,st-vgxy61.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/st,st-vgxy61.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `STMicroelectronics VGxy61 HDR Global Shutter Sensor Family`, a camera image sensor binding. STMicroelectronics VGxy61 family has a CSI-2 output port. CSI-2 output is a quad lanes 800Mbps per lane. Supported formats are RAW8, RAW10, RAW12, RAW14 and RAW16. Following part number are supported - VG5661 and VG6661 are 1.6 Mpx (1464 x 1104) monochrome and color sensors. Maximum frame rate is 75 fps. - VG5761 and VG6761 are 2.3 Mpx (1944 x 1204) monochrome and color sensors. Maximum frame rate is 60 fps. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `st,st-vgxy61`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `VCORE-supply`, `VDDIO-supply`, `VANA-supply`, `reset-gpios`, `st,strobe-gpios-polarity`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `clocks`, `VCORE-supply`, `VDDIO-supply`, `VANA-supply`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `VANA-supply`, `VCORE-supply`, `VDDIO-supply`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `data-lanes`, `port`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `VCORE-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `VDDIO-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `VANA-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Benjamin Mugnier <benjamin.mugnier@foss.st.com>, Sylvain Petinot <sylvain.petinot@foss.st.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 9 top-level properties and 12 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/st,st-vgxy61.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/thine,thp7312.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/thine,thp7312.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `THine THP7312`, a Linux media subsystem Devicetree binding. The THP7312 is a standalone ISP controlled over i2c, and is capable of various image processing and correction functions, including 3A control. It can be connected to CMOS image sensors from various vendors, supporting both MIPI CSI-2 and parallel interfaces. It can also output on either MIPI CSI-2 or parallel. The hardware is capable of transmitting and receiving MIPI interleaved data streams with data types or multiple virtual channel identifiers. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `vddcore-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vhtermrx-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddtx-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddhost-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddcmos-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddgpio-0-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `vddgpio-1-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Paul Elder <paul.elder@@ideasonboard.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/thp7312.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 16 top-level properties and 22 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/thine,thp7312.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
