# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/toshiba,et8ek8.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/toshiba,et8ek8.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Toshiba et8ek8 5MP sensor`, a camera image sensor binding. Toshiba et8ek8 5MP sensor is an image sensor found in Nokia N900 device It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `toshiba,et8ek8`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vana-supply`, `clocks`, `reset-gpios`, `flash-leds`, `port`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `vana-supply`, `clocks`, `reset-gpios`, `port`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `port`, `reg`, `reset-gpios`, `vana-supply`. The node acts as a I2C client node selected by parent bus address cells. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `vana-supply` links power rails or power-domain providers; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates; `reset-gpios` controls hardware reset, enable, or power-down sequencing; `port` describes media-controller OF graph links and endpoint topology. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are `endpoint`, `port`. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Pavel Machek <pavel@ucw.cz>, Sakari Ailus <sakari.ailus@iki.fi>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are `allOf`. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/i2c/et8ek8/et8ek8_driver.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/ti/omap/omap3-n900.dts`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: composition is closed by `unevaluatedProperties: false`. This file has 7 top-level properties and 8 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/toshiba,et8ek8.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/i2c/toshiba,tc358746.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `Toshiba TC358746 Parallel to MIPI CSI2 Bridge`, a video receiver or media bus bridge binding. The Toshiba TC358746 converts a parallel video stream into a MIPI CSI-2 stream. The direction can be either parallel-in -> csi-out or csi-in -> parallel-out The chip is programmable through I2C and SPI but the SPI interface is only supported in parallel-in -> csi-out mode. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mdp3-fg.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted compatible forms and covers 0 compatible tokens: no explicit compatible values. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `clocks`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `clocks`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `reg`. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit graph/child nodes discovered. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mdp3-tdshp.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mdp3-tdshp.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek Media Data Path 3 Two-Dimensional Sharpness`, a SoC media processing or capture engine binding. Two-Dimensional Sharpness (TDSHP) is a Media Profile Path 3 (MDP3) component used to perform image edge sharpening and enhance vividness and contrast. It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted compatible forms and covers 0 compatible tokens: no explicit compatible values. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `clocks`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `clocks`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clocks`, `compatible`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`, `reg`. The node acts as a platform or MMIO media engine node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `reg` carries the I2C address or MMIO register range; `clocks` binds clock inputs, outputs, or rates. Patterned child schemas are none; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit graph/child nodes discovered. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: Matthias Brugger <matthias.bgg@gmail.com>, Moudy Ho <moudy.ho@mediatek.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: no direct in-tree driver match found by compatible scan. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: no in-tree DTS users found by compatible scan. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 4 top-level properties and 4 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mdp3-tdshp.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mt8195-jpegdec.yaml

**Purpose:** `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mt8195-jpegdec.yaml` is a Linux Devicetree YAML schema for `MediaTek JPEG Decoder`, a memory-to-memory media codec or image processor binding. MediaTek JPEG Decoder is the JPEG decode hardware present in MediaTek SoCs It is declarative ABI documentation and validation input, not executable driver code. Its job is to constrain how board DTS files describe media hardware so V4L2, media-controller, remote-control, codec, bridge, sensor, clock, regulator, GPIO, DMA, and platform/I2C driver code can discover the expected resources.

**Important APIs/types/functions:** The public API surface is the schema contract: `$id`, `$schema`, `title`, `maintainers`, `properties`, `required`, composition keys, examples, and any OF graph or child-node patterns. `compatible` uses single `const` and covers 1 compatible token: `mediatek,mt8195-jpgdec`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `power-domains`, `iommus`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `ranges`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `power-domains`, `iommus`, `ranges`; required properties found across nested schemas include `clock-names`, `clocks`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `iommus`, `power-domains`, `ranges`, `reg`. The node acts as a Devicetree schema fragment or parent-managed node. Important property roles: `compatible` selects the OF match table entry and stable hardware ABI; `power-domains` links power rails or power-domain providers; `iommus` connects media buffers, firmware, or DMA/IOMMU resources; `#address-cells` enables validated child nodes or provider specifiers; `#size-cells` enables validated child nodes or provider specifiers. Patterned child schemas are `^jpgdec@[0-9a-f]+$`; graph or child-node properties discovered in nested schemas are no explicit graph/child nodes discovered. Numeric/item constraints include no explicit numeric min/max/item limits collected.

**Control flow:** Control flow is dt-schema evaluation rather than imperative logic. `dt_binding_check` loads the core meta-schema, resolves every `$ref`, applies `properties`, `required`, item/count limits, and composition branches, validates inline examples, and enforces the unknown-property policy. At runtime the YAML is not executed: the compiled DTB node is matched through the driver's OF table, and media graph parsing follows `port`/`ports` endpoints to connect sensors, bridges, serializers, deserializers, receivers, codecs, and SoC capture or processing blocks. Resource acquisition then proceeds through standard helpers for I2C/platform devices, regulators, clocks, resets, GPIOs, IRQs, DMA/IOMMU, firmware, and child remote buses when those properties are present.

**State and persistence behavior:** The schema has no mutable runtime state and writes no persistent data. Its persistent effect is ABI stability: accepted compatible strings, required supplies/clocks/resets/IRQs, endpoint lane layouts, child-node names, remote-I2C topology, and MMIO or I2C address shapes become long-lived DTB contracts. Runtime state such as video streaming, link training, sensor mode selection, codec sessions, remote serializer state, radio tuning, frame buffers, and power sequencing belongs to the matching kernel drivers and hardware.

**Dependencies and integration points:** Maintainers: kyrie wu <kyrie.wu@mediatek.corp-partner.google.com>. Direct schema dependencies are no external `$ref` beyond the core meta-schema. Top-level composition/conditional keys are none. Integration points include Devicetree core schemas, media/video interface schemas, graph endpoint schemas, the V4L2 async notifier and media-controller graph, camera sensor and bridge drivers, platform codec or image-processing drivers, remote-control/radio helpers where applicable, regulator/clk/reset/GPIO/IRQ/DMA/IOMMU subsystems, and bus-standard I2C or platform device probing. Compatible scan signals found driver-side files: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/platform/mediatek/jpeg/mtk_jpeg_core.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/platform/mediatek/jpeg/mtk_jpeg_dec_hw.c`. In-tree DTS scan signals found board users: `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt8195.dtsi`. The file includes 1 example block for schema validation.

**Risks:** Risks center on Devicetree ABI drift and media graph mismatches. Changing compatible values or fallback order can break driver binding; renaming supplies, clocks, GPIOs, interrupts, reset lines, `#*-cells`, or child nodes can break probe-time resource lookup; tightening endpoint lane counts or port numbering can reject valid boards or silently disconnect media links. Serializer/deserializer bindings add risk around remote I2C aliases, PoC supplies, GPIO forwarding, and link topology. Codec and SoC processing bindings add risk around clocks, power domains, IOMMU/DMA resources, and firmware/mailbox style dependencies. The closure profile is: top-level unknown properties are rejected by `additionalProperties: false`. This file has 6 top-level properties and 10 distinct nested property names, so schema cleanups should be checked against all compatibles and real DTS users, not only the filename's nominal device.

**Test signals:** Primary validation signals are `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mt8195-jpegdec.yaml` and `make dtbs_check` for boards instantiating each compatible. Useful negative tests remove required regulators/clocks/resets/IRQs, use invalid endpoint `data-lanes` or port numbering, misspell vendor properties, and exercise every compatible branch. Runtime integration is indicated by successful driver probe, correct media-controller entity/link topology, valid V4L2 async subdevice binding, stable power sequencing, clean stream-on/stream-off for sensors and bridges, successful codec or image-processing queue setup for SoC blocks, and no dtbs_check regressions for existing DTS examples.
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/media/mediatek,mt8195-jpegdec.yaml -->
